Dear lawmakers, the law of Torba is not a term that is legally mentioned in our Constitution, nor in the Civil Code. However, the public is called the law of torba. Why is it called the law of torba? There are two kinds of law-making techniques: one, the standard law-making technique; the second, the extraordinary law-making technique. The usual law-making technique comes to the commission on a normal bill or a proposal on the law, the relevant commission argues it, sends it to the General Assembly. And the way he's doing the extraordinary law, that's the bag law that we're talking about right now. What's this? So far, it's an intensively applied method starting in 2002. A total of seven cases have been applied to the law in the 21st term, the 22nd term has been applied 29 times, the 23rd term has been applied 45 times, but the 24th term -- we have completed more or less a year -- most of the laws that have been passed so far come to parliament as a bag law. What's the practical benefit and harm when this comes to Parliament as a bag law? The harm is that in commissions that are irrelevant, in non-private commissions, that offer is discussed and legalized. It also creates the possibility of making too many mistakes. That law causes both opposition parties to become lawless without being examined by NGOs or controlled by NGOs, which is against the parliamentary regime. Despite all these arrangements and international contracts, our nearly 9 million disabled citizens are living without those basic rights. For example, public institutions and institutions under Law 657 are obliged to employ as many as 3 per cent of their staff as disabled persons, but public institutions do not comply with that obligation. The problem has only been reduced by the state to a welfare issue. The right of our disabled to live and work in society under equal conditions and equal opportunities is ignored. Dear lawmakers, one of the basic criteria for whether a state is a modern state is the approach to disadvantaged groups such as our disabled citizens. Today, more than 80 per cent of our disabled citizens are unable to benefit from education, more than 90 per cent of their work and skills. Only 5 per cent can work. On top of that, the sidewalks were made as if there were no disabled people in this country. Transport is arranged as if disabled people should never travel. Overpasses are designed as if the disabled should never cross the street. Dear lawmakers, this arrangement was made in 2005 so that our disabled citizens could benefit from all their rights, provide transportation, relax on sidewalks, parks, and set up for a seven-year period. For seven years, in major cities, especially Istanbul and Ankara, sidewalks have been ripped out, rebuilt, roads have been redacted, money poured out, but while these were being done, it was treated as if there were no disabled citizens in this country. Now it's thought to be rectified by eight years. tbmm-2012-06-28sit08spe0459par0467	Mr. President, dear lawmakers, I've been compelled to take a stand on this clause, and I don't really have much to say about the substance. Since the bag is being held as a fundamental law, I would like to express my views on Article 39, since I cannot speak on Article 39. Article 39 makes a significant change in the Law of the Court. The Law of the Court is a law adopted by the Grand National Assembly of Turkey at the end of 2010. The Court's Law defines the Court's audit in parallel with the Law on Financial Management and Control No. 5018. The definitions of the Court's audit are in both laws in detail, one of which is regularity control. The regularity control has two subtitles: the first is financial control, and the other is compliance control. Financial audit is the Court's audit on whether the financial tables are true as a result of the assessment of the results of the institutions' activity on financial transactions. The appropriate audit is the audit of public administration's income, expenses and property if it is in accordance with the law. The sum of these two audits is called the regulation control. With what is now being brought in, regularity inspection, therefore the Court's audit on behalf of the Grand National Assembly of Turkey is being massacred, and that is what that means. The definition of regularity control is now in the name of the executive body of the Turkish National Assembly. If the Court is conducting an audit on behalf of the Grand National Assembly of Turkey, the executive body's regulations, regulations, the Council of Ministers, the regulations such as the circuses do not bind the Court; the Court will make a law enforcement audit. If the Court will supervise a general notification issued by a ministry, a circus, if the Court will supervise whether other public institutions or the ministry obey it, if the Court will take it as legislation that should be obeyed by the Court itself, then the Court will not be auditing it on behalf of the Parliament. The Court is not bound by any procedure of the executive body; it is tasked to oversee the legality of all operations. The matter completely eliminates it, completely eliminates it. The provisions of Court Law 6085 concerning control are the articles that we support, which are modern. All over the world, where we see the example, in all developed countries, in all countries where the Court exists, and after many years, Turkey has taken a serious step towards adapting to this control system, which is a serious step, despite its shortcomings. Now, a ministry is going to publish a statement, and if it has been spent according to the order, the Court will not supervise it, it is against the law. It will not be able to comment.
